Polyfills in JavaScript are pieces of code (usually written in JavaScript) that provide the functionality that you expect to be available in the user's browser, but which might not be there. They are essentially fallbacks that implement modern JavaScript features in older browsers that do not natively support them.\n\n### Key Characteristics of Polyfills:\n1. **Backward Compatibility**: Polyfills ensure that your code works in older or less capable browsers by mimicking the behavior of newer features.\n2. **Conditional Loading**: Polyfills are often loaded conditionally based on the browser's capabilities. `current_sum`.\n   - If `current_sum` exceeds `k`, move the `start` pointer to the right until `current_sum` is less than or equal to `k`.\n   - Update `max_length` if the current sub-array length is greater than the previously found maximum length.\n\n3. **Edge Cases**:\n   - If the array is empty, return 0.\n   - If all elements are non-positive and `k` is non-negative, the entire array is a They can support various applications, including file sharing, messaging, and data transfer.\n\n**Differences:**\n- **Role Distribution:** In client-server, servers provide resources and services to clients, while clients request and consume these resources. In P2P, all nodes (peers) act as both clients and servers, sharing resources directly.\n- **Centralization:** Client-server is centralized, with servers managing and controlling resources. P2P is decentralized, with no single point of control.\n- **Scalability:** P2P is more scalable as new peers can join without overloading a central server. Client-server can become a bottleneck as the number of clients increases.\n\n### Question 2: What are the main differences between cellular networks and WiFi networks in terms of range, bandwidth, and mobility? Additionally, if multiple sub-arrays have the same length, return the one with the smallest sum. To determine the longest sub-array of `a` that sums to less than or equal to a given value `k`, and to return the one with the smallest sum in case of a tie, we can use a sliding window approach. This method is efficient and handles all edge cases.\n\nHere’s how you can implement it in Python:\n\n```python\ndef longest_subarray_with_sum_k(a, k):\n    if not a:\n        return []\n\n    n = len(a)\n    max_length = 0\n    best_sum = float('inf')\n    best_start = -1\n    current_sum = 0\n    start = 0\n\n    for end in range(n):\n        current_sum += a[end]\n\n        while current_sum > k:\n            current_sum -= a[start]\n            start += 1\n\n        if end - start + 1 > max_length or (end - start + 1 == max_length and current_sum < best_sum):\n            max_length = end - start + 1\n            best_sum = current_sum\n            best_start = start\n\n    return a[best_start:best_start + max_length]\n\n# Example usage:\na = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]\nk = 10\nprint(longest_subarray_with_sum_k(a, k))  # Output: [1, 2, 3, 4]\n```\n\n### Explanation:\n1. **Initialization**:\n   - `max_length` to keep track of the length of the longest valid sub-array The resources can include web apps, databases, storage accounts, and more.\n- **Purpose**: Resource groups help you manage and organize your resources in a structured way. You can apply access control, tags, and policies to the entire group, which simplifies management and governance.\n- **Relationship to Web Applications**: When you create a web application in Azure App Service, you first create or select a resource group. All resources related to the web application (e.g., the app itself, associated databases, storage accounts) are typically placed in the same resource group. This makes it easier to manage and delete all related resources together.\n\n#### 2. **App Service Plans**\n- **Definition**: An App Service Plan defines the set of compute resources for a web app, API, or mobile app. It specifies the location, size, and features of the virtual machines that host your app.\n- **Purpose**: App Service Plans are used to manage the hosting environment for your applications. They determine the cost, performance, and scalability of your app.\n- **Relationship to Web Applications**: Each web application in Azure App Service is associated with an App Service Plan. Multiple web applications can share the same App Service Plan, which can help you manage costs and resources more efficiently. You can scale the App Service Plan (e.g., increase the number of instances, change the pricing tier) to meet the performance and scalability needs of your applications.\n\n#### 3. **Web Applications**\n- **Definition**: A web application is the actual application you deploy to Azure App Service. Large problems can often be divided into smaller ones, which can then be solved at the same time. It enables the execution of multiple program instructions or tasks simultaneously, enhancing computational power and efficiency.\n\n### Why Parallel Computing?\n\n1. **Increased Performance**: Parallel computing can significantly reduce the time required to complete complex tasks by distributing the workload across multiple processors.\n2. **Scalability**: As the size of the problem grows, parallel computing can scale up to handle larger datasets or more complex computations by adding more processors.\n3. **Resource Utilization**: It makes efficient use of available hardware resources, leading to better utilization of computational power.\n4. **Cost-Effectiveness**: For large-scale computations, parallel computing can be more cost-effective than using a single, extremely powerful machine.\n\n### Goals of Parallel Computing\n\n1. **Speedup**: To reduce the time required to solve a problem compared to a sequential approach.\n2. **Efficiency**: To utilize computational resources effectively, ensuring that processors are not idle.\n3. **Scalability**: To handle increases in problem size or complexity without a proportional increase in computation time.\n4. **Reliability**: To ensure that computations are accurate and consistent, even in the presence of hardware failures.\n5. **Portability**: To develop algorithms and applications that can run on different parallel
